Hypercholesterolemia</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/sites/books/NBK174884/table/hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="col" colspan="1" headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Gene(s)</th><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_2" rowspan="2" scope="col" colspan="1" headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_2" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">DiffDx Disorder</th><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_3" rowspan="2" scope="col" colspan="1" headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_3" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">MOI</th><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_4" colspan="2" scope="colgroup" rowspan="1" style="text-align:center;vertical-align:middle;">Features of DiffDx Disorder</th></tr><tr><th headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_4" id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_2_1" colspan="1" scope="colgroup" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Similar to FH</th><th headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_4" id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_2_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Distinguishing from FH</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>ABCG5</i>
<br />
<i>ABCG8</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/stsl/">Sitosterolemia</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_4 hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_2_1" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Xanthomas; susceptibility to early-onset CAD. Some persons may present w/&#x02191; plasma cholesterol/LDL-C (often presents in childhood).</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_4 hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_2_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Hematologic abnormalities (hemolytic anemia, thrombocytopenia, stomatocytes); xanthomas in childhood (particularly in absence of hyperlipidemia in parents); poor response to statins</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="row" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>APOE</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">p.Leu167del-related AD hypercholesterolemia&#x000a0;<sup>1</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AD</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_4 hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_2_1" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Persons may present w/&#x02191; LDL &#x00026; &#x02191; risk of CAD.</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_4 hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_2_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Splenomegaly, sea-blue histiocytosis, thrombocytopenia, &#x00026; &#x02191; triglycerides reported in some p.Leu167del heterozygotes</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Hyperlipoproteinemia type III (<a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/familial/">familial</a> dysbetalipoproteinemia [FD]) (OMIM <a href="https://omim.org/entry/617347" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">617347</a>)</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR<br />AD&#x000a0;<sup>2</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_4 hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_2_1" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Persons w/predisposing <i>APOE</i> variants are at risk of developing xanthomas (more commonly cutaneous &#x00026; tuberous) &#x00026; premature CAD. Most common <i>APOE</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genotype/">genotype</a> assoc w/FD: homozygosity for E2 <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/allele/">allele</a> (p.Arg176Cys); however, &#x0003e;30 <i>APOE</i> variants have been assoc w/FD.&#x000a0;<sup>2</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_4 hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_2_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Persons w/FD are at risk of developing &#x02191; triglycerides.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>CYP27A1</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/ctx/">Cerebrotendinous xanthomatosis (CTX)</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_4 hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_2_1" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Xanthomas</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_4 hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_2_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Dementia, ataxia, &#x00026; cataracts; normal LDL-C</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>LIPA</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/lal-def/">Lysosomal acid lipase deficiency</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_4 hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_2_1" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">&#x02191; LDL-C; risk of CAD</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_1_4 hd_h_hyperchol.T.genetic_disorders_with_featu_1_1_2_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>In infantile onset (Wolman disease): &#x02191; triglycerides, malnutrition, hepatosplenomegaly, liver disease, adrenal cortical insufficiency</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>In adult onset (cholesterol-ester storage disease): hepatosplenomegaly &#x00026;/or &#x02191; liver enzymes, &#x02191; triglycerides</div></li></ul>
</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">AD = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-dominant/">autosomal dominant</a>; AR = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a>; CAD = coronary artery disease; DiffDx = differential diagnosis; FH = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/familial/">familial</a> hypercholesterolemia; LDL = low-density lipoprotein; LDL-C = low-density lipoprotein cholesterol; MOI = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/mode-of-inheritance/">mode of inheritance</a></p></div></dd><dt>1. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.5.1"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.cenarro.2016.2113">Cenarro et al [2016]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>2. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.5.2"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.khalil.2021.11">Khalil et al [2021]</a>
</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div><p>Hypercholesterolemia secondary to acquired conditions including obesity, diabetes mellitus, obstructive liver disease, hypothyroidism, drugs (e.g., steroids), or kidney disease can also be associated with laboratory findings similar to those of FH [<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.goldberg.2011.s1">Goldberg et al 2011</a>].</p></div><div id="hyperchol.Management"><h2 id="_hyperchol_Management_">Management</h2><div id="hyperchol.Evaluations_Following_Initial"><h3>Evaluations Following Initial Diagnosis</h3><p>To establish the extent of disease and needs of an individual diagnosed with <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/familial/">familial</a> hypercholesterolemia (FH), the evaluations summarized in <a href="/sites/books/NBK174884/table/hyperchol.T.recommended_evaluations_foll/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobhypercholTrecommendedevaluationsfoll">Table 6</a> (if not performed as part of the evaluation that led to the diagnosis) are recommended.</p><div id="hyperchol.T.recommended_evaluations_foll" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 6. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p>Recommended Evaluations Following Initial Diagnosis in Individuals with Familial Hypercholesterolemia</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/sites/books/NBK174884/table/hyperchol.T.recommended_evaluations_foll/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__hyperchol.T.recommended_evaluations_foll_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_evaluations_foll_1_1_1_1" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">System/Concern</th><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_evaluations_foll_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Evaluation</th><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_evaluations_foll_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Comment</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_evaluations_foll_1_1_1_1" rowspan="4" scope="row" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Cardiovascular</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_evaluations_foll_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Measurement of pre-treatment lipid values when possible: TC, LDL-C, HDL-C, triglycerides, lipoprotein(a)</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_evaluations_foll_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_evaluations_foll_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="col" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Consultation w/lipid specialist or clinician w/expertise in FH</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_evaluations_foll_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_evaluations_foll_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Consider noninvasive imaging modalities (e.g., measurement of carotid intima-media thickness) to help inform treatment decisions, esp in those w/HoFH or AR FH.</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_evaluations_foll_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Recommended in some guidelines&#x000a0;<sup>1</sup></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_evaluations_foll_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="col" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Eval for concurrent illnesses (kidney disease, obstructive liver disease, acute myocardial infarction, hypothyroidism) that can affect lipid values</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_evaluations_foll_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_evaluations_foll_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Genetic</b>
<br />
<b>counseling</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_evaluations_foll_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">By genetics professionals&#x000a0;<sup>2</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_evaluations_foll_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">To obtain a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pedigree/">pedigree</a> &#x00026; inform affected persons &#x00026; their families re nature, MOI, &#x00026; implications of FH to facilitate medical &#x00026; personal decision making</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">AR = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a>; FH = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/familial/">familial</a> hypercholesterolemia; HDL-C = high-density lipoprotein cholesterol; HoFH = FH resulting from <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> (<a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/homozygous/">homozygous</a> or <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/compound-heterozygous/">compound heterozygous</a>) pathogenic variants in <i>APOB, LDLR</i>, or <i>PCSK9</i>; LDL-C = low-density lipoprotein cholesterol; MOI = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/mode-of-inheritance/">mode of inheritance</a>; TC = total cholesterol</p></div></dd><dt>1. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.6.1"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.martin.2013.e263">Martin et al [2013]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>2. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.6.2"><p class="no_margin">Clinical geneticist, certified genetic counselor, certified genetic nurse, genetics advanced practice provider (nurse practitioner or physician assistant)</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div></div><div id="hyperchol.Treatment_of_Manifestations"><h3>Treatment of Manifestations</h3><div id="hyperchol.Adults_with_FH"><h4>Adults with FH</h4><p>All individuals with FH should be considered at high risk for coronary artery disease (CAD) and should be treated actively to lower cholesterol levels. Note that standard Framingham or other risk classification schemes are not applicable [<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.goldberg.2011.s1">Goldberg et al 2011</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.hopkins.2011.s9">Hopkins et al 2011</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.stone.2014.2889">Stone et al 2014</a>]. The most current recommendations (summarized here) for the management of FH used in the United States are from the American Heart Association [<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.gidding.2015.2167">Gidding et al 2015</a>] (<a href="https://www.ahajournals.org/doi/full/10.1161/cir.0000000000000297" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">full text</a>) and largely reflect earlier recommendations from the National Lipid Association.</p><div id="hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 7a. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p>Treatment of Manifestations in Adults with Familial Hypercholesterolemia</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/sites/books/NBK174884/table/hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Manifestation/Concern</th><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Treatment</th><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Considerations/Other</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1" rowspan="5" scope="row" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Hyperlipidemia</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Regular physical activity</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Healthy diet (&#x02193; saturated fat intake, &#x02191; intake of soluble fiber to 10-20 g/day)</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Weight control &#x00026;/or weight loss as needed</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Statin therapy to &#x02193; LDL-C level by &#x02265;50% or to &#x0003c;100 mg/dL (&#x0003c;2.6 mmol/L).&#x000a0;<sup>1</sup> Dose should be titrated every few mos to highest tolerated dose.</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Many guidelines suggest a target LDL-C of &#x0003c;100 mg/dL even in those w/o CAD due to lifelong high LDL-C.&#x000a0;<sup>2</sup> If there is a history of CAD or stroke, target LDL-C levels should be lower (LDL-C &#x0003c;70 mg/dL).</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="col" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Consider referral to lipid specialist w/expertise in FH if LDL-C levels cannot be reduced by &#x02265;50% or to &#x0003c;100 mg/dL w/maximal medical therapy over 6 mos.</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Addl treatments if targeted LDL-C level is not achieved:
<ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Ezetimibe</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Bile acid sequestrants</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>PCSK9 inhibitors</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Bempedoic acid</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">The potential benefit of multidrug regimens should be weighed against &#x02191; cost &#x00026; potential for adverse effects &#x00026; &#x02193; adherence.<br />Note: Niacin was used historically but is generally not favored before other options (<a href="/sites/books/NBK174884/table/hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobhypercholTcurrentrecommendeddrugthe">Table 7b</a>) have been exhausted.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="col" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">More aggressive treatment may be needed in those w/the following CAD risk factors:
<ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Clinically evident CAD or other atherosclerotic CVD; goal is LDL-C level of &#x0003c;70 mg/dL (&#x0003c;1.8 mmol/L)</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Diabetes mellitus or metabolic syndrome</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Family history of very early CAD (CAD diagnosed in men age &#x0003c;45 yrs; women age &#x0003c;55 yrs)</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Current smoking</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>High lipoprotein(a) (&#x02265;50 mg/dL [&#x02265;1.3 mmol/L] using an isoform-insensitive assay)</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="row" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>CVD&#x000a0;/ Compounding</b>
<br />
<b>risk factors</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Consider low-dose aspirin (75-81 mg/day) in those w/CAD, prior stroke, or at high risk for CAD or stroke.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Smoking cessation</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Measure serum lipoprotein(a).</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Treatment for diabetes mellitus</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Treatment of hypertension</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Treat blood pressure to 140/90 mm Hg (or 130/80 mm Hg in those w/diabetes mellitus).</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">CAD = coronary artery disease; CVD = cardiovascular disease; FH = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/familial/">familial</a> hypercholesterolemia; LDL-C = low-density lipoprotein cholesterol</p></div></dd><dt>1. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.7a.1"><p class="no_margin"><a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.hopkins.2011.s9">Hopkins et al [2011]</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.nordestgaard.2013.3478">Nordestgaard et al [2013]</a></p></div></dd><dt>2. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.7a.2"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.gidding.2015.2167">Gidding et al [2015]</a>
</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div><div id="hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 7b. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p>Current Recommended Drug Therapies for Adults with Familial Hypercholesterolemia</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/sites/books/NBK174884/table/hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_1" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Class</th><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Primary (1<sup>O</sup>) and Secondary (2<sup>O</sup>) Mechanism of Action</th><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">LDL-Lowering Response</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Statins</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">&#x02191; LDLR activity (1<sup>O</sup>)<br />Inhibition of cholesterol biosynthesis through HMG-CoA reductase</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">50%&#x000a0;<sup>1,&#x000a0;2</sup></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><b>Cholesterol absorption inhibitors</b> (ezetimibe)</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">&#x02193; cholesterol absorption (1<sup>O</sup>)<br />&#x02191; LDLR activity (2<sup>O</sup>)</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">15%&#x000a0;<sup>1,&#x000a0;3</sup></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><b>MTP inhibitor</b><br />(lomitapide)&#x000a0;<sup>4</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">&#x02193; microsomal triglyceride transfer protein activity&#x000a0;(1<sup>O</sup>)<br />Inhibition of LDL production (2<sup>O</sup>)</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">50%&#x000a0;<sup>5</sup></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><b>PCSK9 inhibitors</b><br />(alirocumab, evolocumab, inclisiran)</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">&#x02193; LDLR degradation</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">50%&#x000a0;<sup>6</sup></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><b>Bile acid sequestrants</b><br />(cholestyramine, colesevelam)</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">&#x02193; bile acid reabsorption (1<sup>O</sup>)<br />&#x02191; LDLR activity (2<sup>O</sup>)</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">15%&#x000a0;<sup>1,&#x000a0;3</sup></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Stanol esters</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">&#x02193; cholesterol absorption (1<sup>O</sup>)<br />&#x02191; LDLR activity (2<sup>O</sup>)</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">10%&#x000a0;<sup>1,&#x000a0;3</sup></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Bempedoic acid</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">&#x02191; LDLR activity (1<sup>O</sup>)<br />Inhibition of cholesterol biosynthesis by inhibiting ATP-citrate lyase</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">15%</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Evinacumab&#x000a0;<sup>4,&#x000a0;7</sup></b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Inhibition of ANGPTL3, which results in &#x02191; lipoprotein lipase activity<br />&#x02193; VLDL</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">44%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">Some guidelines call for the addition of n-3 polyunsaturated fatty acids or fibrates if triglycerides remain elevated after the LDL-C level is controlled.</p></div></dd><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">LDL = low-density lipoprotein; LDL-C = low-density lipoprotein cholesterol; LDLR = low-density lipoprotein receptor</p></div></dd><dt>1. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.7b.1"><p class="no_margin">Often ineffective in HoFH due to <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> pathogenic variants in <i>APOB, LDLR</i>, or <i>PCSK9</i>.</p></div></dd><dt>2. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.7b.2"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.kastelein.2008.1431">Kastelein et al [2008]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>3. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.7b.3"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.rader.2003.1795">Rader et al [2003]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>4. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.7b.4"><p class="no_margin">Approved only for HoFH</p></div></dd><dt>5. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.7b.5"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.cuchel.2014.2146">Cuchel et al [2014]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>6. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.7b.6"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.raal.2012.262">Raal &#x00026; Santos [2012]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>7. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.7b.7"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.gaudet.2024.2422">Gaudet et al [2024]</a>
</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div></div><div id="hyperchol.Children_with_FH"><h4>Children with FH</h4><p>Guidelines for the management of children have been published by multiple national and international organizations [<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.demott.2008">DeMott et al 2008</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.descamps.2011.272">Descamps et al 2011</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.martin.2013.e263">Martin et al 2013</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.de_ferranti.2019.e603">de Ferranti et al 2019</a>] (see <a href="#hyperchol.Published_Guidelines__Consensu">Published Guidelines&#x000a0;/ Consensus Statements</a>).</p><div id="hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 7c. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p>Treatment of Manifestations in Children with Familial Hypercholesterolemia</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/sites/books/NBK174884/table/hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1_1" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Manifestation/Concern</th><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Treatment</th><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Considerations/Other</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1_1" rowspan="4" scope="row" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Hyperlipidemia</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Regular physical activity &#x00026; limited screen time</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Healthy diet: high in fiber, low in saturated fat (&#x02264;7% of calories), avoidance of trans fats, &#x0003c;200 mg/day of dietary cholesterol</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Maintenance of healthy weight</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Statin therapy as first-line agent for most children&#x000a0;<sup>1</sup>
<ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Children w/LDL-C &#x02265;190 mg/dL&#x000a0;<sup>2</sup></div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>In children w/LDL-C 130-189 mg/dL, the initiation of statins depends on assessment of family history, risk factors, &#x00026; comorbidities.</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="2" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Pharmacotherapy is typically considered as early as age 10 yrs, but statins may be used as early as age 8 yrs.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Some experts recommend target LDL-C of &#x02264;130 mg/dL or a 50% reduction.&#x000a0;<sup>3</sup> Others rely on adult LDL-C targets for severe FH of &#x0003c;100 mg/dL.&#x000a0;<sup>4</sup></div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="col" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Consider additional lipid-lowering therapy (ezetimibe, evolocumab, &#x00026; bile acid sequestrants) if target LDL-C is not achieved w/lifestyle modification &#x00026; statins.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="col" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Referral to lipid specialist w/expertise in FH if LDL-C remains &#x02265;130 mg/dL after treatment&#x000a0;<sup>2</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1_1" rowspan="3" scope="row" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Compounding risk factors</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Cessation of smoking &#x00026; exposure to secondhand smoke</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Consider measurement of serum lipoprotein(a).</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Some experts recommend this, but the practice is not universal.&#x000a0;<sup>3,&#x000a0;5</sup></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Treatment for diabetes mellitus</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Treatment of hypertension</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">See AHA scientific statement on cardiovascular risk reduction in high-risk children.&#x000a0;<sup>3</sup></td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">FH = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/familial/">familial</a> hypercholesterolemia; LDL-C = low-density lipoprotein cholesterol</p></div></dd><dt>1. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.7c.1"><p class="no_margin">The safety and efficacy of statins in children is supported by clinical trial data and by long-term observational data [<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.kusters.2014.1055">Kusters et al 2014</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.luirink.2019.1547">Luirink et al 2019</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.vuorio.2019.cd006401">Vuorio et al 2019</a>].</p></div></dd><dt>2. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.7c.2"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.nhlbi.2011.s213">NHLBI [2011]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>3. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.7c.3"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.de_ferranti.2019.e603">de Ferranti et al [2019]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>4. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.7c.4"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.grundy.2019.e1082">Grundy et al [2019]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>5. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.7c.5"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.wiegman.2015.2425">Wiegman et al [2015]</a>
</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div></div><div id="hyperchol.Children_and_Adults_with_HoFH"><h4>Children and Adults with HoFH</h4><p>National Lipid Association guidelines for severe FH (also referred to as HoFH; see <a href="#hyperchol.Nomenclature">Nomenclature</a>) due to <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> pathogenic variants in <i>APOB</i>, <i>LDLR</i>, or <i>PCSK9</i> have been published (<a href="https://www.lipid.org/sites/default/files/articles/familial_hypercholesterolemia_1.pdf" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">full text</a>).</p><div id="hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 7d. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p>Treatment of Manifestations in Children and Adults with Severe Familial Hypercholesterolemia (HoFH)</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/sites/books/NBK174884/table/hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_1" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Manifestation/Concern</th><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Treatment</th><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Considerations/Other</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_1" rowspan="7" scope="row" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Hyperlipidemia</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Referral to lipid specialist w/expertise in FH</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Multi-drug therapy usually required (See <a href="/sites/books/NBK174884/table/hyperchol.T.current_recommended_drug_the/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobhypercholTcurrentrecommendeddrugthe">Table 7b</a>.)</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Evolocumab, alicrocumab (PCSK9 inhibitors)</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>40% mean reduction in LDL-C compared w/placebo; however, persons w/<a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/loss-of-function/">loss-of-function</a> variants saw no response.&#x000a0;<sup>1</sup></div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>To date, only evolocumab has been approved for children w/FH &#x00026; HoFH.&#x000a0;<sup>2</sup></div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">In those w/some residual LDLR activity:
<ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>High-dose statins</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Ezetimibe</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Bile acid-binding resins</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Medications that target the LDLR may be ineffective in persons w/<a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> loss-of function <i>LDLR</i> pathogenic variants.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Lomitapide</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Effective even w/complete loss of LDLR function; though not formally FDA approved for children, should strongly be considered.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Evinacumab (ANGPTL3 inhibitor)&#x000a0;<sup>3</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Recombinant human monoclonal antibody that binds to &#x00026; inhibits ANGPTL3</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Available for adults &#x00026; children &#x02265;5 yrs w/HoFH</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Used in conjunction w/other lipid-lowering therapies</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">LDL apheresis (&#x02264;2x/wk) is often required from a young age.</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Apheresis can &#x02193; LDL-C levels by 80% acutely &#x00026; 30% chronically (weekly or biweekly). Apheresis is offered at a limited number (~40-50) of centers in the US.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Liver transplantation</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Used in rare cases in some centers&#x000a0;<sup>4</sup></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Atherosclerotic CVD</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Severe FH can lead to clinical manifestations of atherosclerosis (incl myocardial infarction) in childhood. Children &#x00026; adults w/clinical atherosclerotic CVD should be evaluated &#x00026; treated by cardiovascular specialists.</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.treatment_of_manifestations_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">CVD = cardiovascular disease; FH = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/familial/">familial</a> hypercholesterolemia; LDL-C = low-density lipoprotein cholesterol; LDLR = low-density lipoprotein receptor</p></div></dd><dt>1. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.7d.1"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.raal.2015.341">Raal et al [2015]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>2. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.7d.2"><p class="no_margin"><a href="https://www.accessdata.fda.gov/drugsatfda_docs/label/2021/125522s29s31lbl.pdf" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">REPATHA<sup>&#x000ae;</sup> (evolocumab) prescribing information</a> (pdf)</p></div></dd><dt>3. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.7d.3"><p class="no_margin"><a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.rosenson.2020.2307">Rosenson et al [2020]</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.gaudet.2024.2422">Gaudet et al [2024]</a>, <a href="https://www.accessdata.fda.gov/drugsatfda_docs/label/2023/761181s001lbl.pdf" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">EVKEEZA<sup>&#x000ae;</sup> (evinacumab-dgnb) prescribing information</a> (pdf)</p></div></dd><dt>4. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.7d.4"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.martinez.2016.504">Martinez et al [2016]</a>
</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div></div></div><div id="hyperchol.Prevention_of_Primary_Manifest"><h3>Prevention of Primary Manifestations</h3><p>To prevent primary manifestations, the following are recommended:</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Reduce saturated fat intake.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Increase intake of soluble fiber to 10-20 g/day.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Increase physical activity.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Do not smoke.</div></li></ul></div><div id="hyperchol.Surveillance"><h3>Surveillance</h3><p>During treatment, individuals of any age with:</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>FH should have lipid levels monitored as recommended;</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Severe FH (HoFH due to <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/homozygous/">homozygous</a> or <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/compound-heterozygous/">compound heterozygous</a> pathogenic variants in <i>APOB</i>, <i>LDLR</i>, or <i>PCSK9</i>, or <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a> FH due to homozygous or compound heterozygous pathogenic variants in <i>LDLRAP1</i>) should be monitored with various imaging modalities (including echocardiogram, CT angiogram, and cardiac catheterization) as recommended [<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.raal.2012.262">Raal &#x00026; Santos 2012</a>].</div></li></ul><p>Additional recommended evaluations include those in <a href="/sites/books/NBK174884/table/hyperchol.T.recommended_surveillance_in/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobhypercholTrecommendedsurveillancein">Table 8</a>.</p><div id="hyperchol.T.recommended_surveillance_in" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 8. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p>Recommended Surveillance in Individuals with Familial Hypercholesterolemia</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/sites/books/NBK174884/table/hyperchol.T.recommended_surveillance_in/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__hyperchol.T.recommended_surveillance_in_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_surveillance_in_1_1_1_1" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">System/Concern</th><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_surveillance_in_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Evaluation</th><th id="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_surveillance_in_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Comment</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_surveillance_in_1_1_1_1" rowspan="3" scope="row" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Cardiovascular</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_surveillance_in_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Measurement of lipid levels: TC, LDL-C, HDL-C, triglycerides, lipoprotein(a)</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_surveillance_in_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>In children: Begin at age 2 yrs &#x00026; monitor closely. At a minimum reassess cholesterol levels between age 9-11 yrs.&#x000a0;<sup>1</sup></div><div>Some experts advocate for screening children for &#x02191; lipoprotein(a), but this practice is not universal.&#x000a0;<sup>1,</sup>&#x000a0;<sup>2</sup></div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>In adults: The interval of follow-up testing TBD by care team &#x00026; based on presence of other risk factors</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_surveillance_in_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Consider noninvasive imaging modalities (e.g., measurement of carotid intima-media thickness) to help inform treatment decisions.</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_surveillance_in_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">In children: Currently, the routine use of imaging (e.g., measurement of carotid intima-media thickness) is not recommended.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_surveillance_in_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="col" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Identify modifiable risk factors: smoking, sedentary behavior, hypertension, diabetes, &#x00026; obesity.</td><td headers="hd_h_hyperchol.T.recommended_surveillance_in_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">HDL-C = high-density lipoprotein cholesterol; LDL-C = low-density lipoprotein cholesterol; TBD = to be determined; TC = total cholesterol</p></div></dd><dt>1. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.8.1"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.de_ferranti.2019.e603">de Ferranti et al [2019]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>2. </dt><dd><div id="hyperchol.TF.8.2"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.wiegman.2015.2425">Wiegman et al [2015]</a>
</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div></div><div id="hyperchol.AgentsCircumstances_to_Avoid"><h3>Agents/Circumstances to Avoid</h3><p>The following should be avoided:</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Smoking</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>High intake of saturated and trans unsaturated fat</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Sedentary lifestyle</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Obesity</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Hypertension</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Type II diabetes mellitus</div></li></ul></div><div id="hyperchol.Evaluation_of_Relatives_at_Ris"><h3>Evaluation of Relatives at Risk</h3><p>The CDC has classified FH as a Tier 1 condition, indicating a significant benefit from performing family-based cascade screening. Cholesterol testing, with or without molecular testing in relatives of affected persons with FH, can be used to identify individuals with FH and provide them with lifesaving treatment. Early diagnosis and treatment of relatives at risk for FH can reduce morbidity and mortality [<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.knowles.2017.381">Knowles et al 2017</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.sturm.2018.662">Sturm et al 2018</a>].</p><p>The genetic status of at-risk family members can be clarified by EITHER of the following:</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Molecular genetic testing if the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>(s) in the family are known;</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Measurement of low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C) level if the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>(s) in the family are not known.</div></li></ul><p>In children with a family history of FH, non-fasting lipid level should be measured by age two years. If lipid level is borderline, measure LDL-C level. An LDL-C level of &#x0003e;130 mg/dL in a child is suspicious for FH in the setting of a known family history of FH, and an LDL-C level of &#x0003e;160 mg/dL is relatively specific for FH.</p><p>See <a href="#hyperchol.Related_Genetic_Counseling_Iss">Genetic Counseling</a> for issues related to testing of at-risk relatives for <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genetic-counseling/">genetic counseling</a> purposes.</p></div><div id="hyperchol.Pregnancy_Management"><h3>Pregnancy Management</h3><p>Pregnant women should incorporate all the other recommended lifestyle changes, including low saturated and trans unsaturated fat intake, no smoking, and high dietary soluble fiber intake.</p><p>Pharmacologic treatment during pregnancy:</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Statins are contraindicated in pregnancy because of concerns for teratogenicity; women with FH who are considering a pregnancy should be counseled of this risk and statins should be discontinued prior to conception. The use of statins during human pregnancy has not been definitively associated with adverse fetal outcome; however, the role of cholesterol in embryologic development has led to theoretic concerns about the effect of these medications on a developing fetus and a recommendation that alternative medications be considered during pregnancy and lactation. Nursing mothers should not take statins.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Bile acid-binding resins (colesevelam, cholestyramine) are generally considered safe (Class B for pregnancy). Based primarily on animal studies, cholestyramine use during pregnancy has not been associated with an increased risk of fetal anomalies. However, use of cholestyramine could theoretically cause depletion of maternal fat-soluble vitamins, including vitamin K.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>LDL apheresis is also occasionally used.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Use of PCSK9 inhibitors, ezetimibe, lomitapide, and bempedoic acid during pregnancy has not been well studied.</div></li></ul></div><div id="hyperchol.Therapies_Under_Investigation"><h3>Therapies Under Investigation</h3><p>Search <a href="https://clinicaltrials.gov/" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">ClinicalTrials.gov</a> in the US and <a href="https://www.clinicaltrialsregister.eu/ctr-search/search" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">EU Clinical Trials Register</a> in Europe for access to information on clinical studies for a wide range of diseases and conditions.</p></div></div><div id="hyperchol.Genetic_Counseling"><h2 id="_hyperchol_Genetic_Counseling_">Genetic Counseling</h2><p>
<i>Genetic counseling is the process of providing individuals and families with
information on the nature, mode(s) of inheritance, and implications of genetic disorders to help them
make informed medical and personal decisions. The following section deals with genetic
risk assessment and the use of family history and genetic testing to clarify genetic
status for family members; it is not meant to address all personal, cultural, or
ethical issues that may arise or to substitute for consultation with a genetics
professional</i>. &#x02014;ED.</p><div id="hyperchol.Mode_of_Inheritance"><h3>Mode of Inheritance</h3><p><i>APOB</i>-, <i>LDLR</i>-, and <i>PCSK9</i>-related <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/familial/">familial</a> hypercholesterolemia (FH) are inherited in an <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-dominant/">autosomal dominant</a> manner. If an individual has <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> (<a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/homozygous/">homozygous</a> or <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/compound-heterozygous/">compound heterozygous</a>) pathogenic variants in one of these three genes &#x02013; a condition referred to as homozygous FH or HoFH &#x02013; the presentation becomes more severe with earlier onset of features. The spectrum of severity in HoFH depends on the genes affected and impact of the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> on protein function [<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.trinder.2020a.390">Trinder et al 2020a</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.trinder.2020b.515">Trinder et al 2020b</a>]. Some individuals with FH are heterozygous for pathogenic variants in two different FH-related genes, which may have an additive effect on the severity of FH [<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.kamar.2021.572045">Kamar et al 2021</a>].</p><p><i>LDLRAP1</i>-related FH (reported to have a presentation similar to <i>LDLR</i>-related HoFH) is caused by <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> pathogenic variants and is inherited in an <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a> manner.</p></div><div id="hyperchol.APOB_LDLR_and_PCSK9Related_FH"><h3><i>APOB</i>-, <i>LDLR</i>-, and <i>PCSK9</i>-Related FH &#x02013; Risk to Family Members</h3><p>
<b>Parents of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a></b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Almost all individuals with FH resulting from a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> in <i>APOB</i>, <i>LDLR</i>, or <i>PCSK9</i> have an affected parent.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The proportion of probands with FH who have a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/de-novo/"><i>de novo</i></a> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> is unknown but appears to be very low.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Recommendations for the parents of the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> include the following:</div><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Measurement of cholesterol (recommended for both the parents of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> with a known <i>APOB</i>, <i>LDLR</i>, or <i>PCSK9</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> or with a clinical diagnosis of FH in the absence of a known FH-causing pathogenic variant) [<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.knowles.2017.381">Knowles et al 2017</a>] (See Management, <a href="#hyperchol.Evaluation_of_Relatives_at_Ris">Evaluation of Relatives at Risk</a>.)</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Targeted <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/molecular-genetic-testing/">molecular genetic testing</a> if an <i>APOB</i>, <i>LDLR</i>, or <i>PCSK9</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> has been identified in the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a></div></li></ul></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> has a known <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> that is not identified in either parent and parental identity testing has confirmed biological maternity and paternity, the following possibilities should be considered:</div><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> has a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/de-novo/"><i>de novo</i></a> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> inherited a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> from a parent with <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/germline/">germline</a> (or somatic and germline) <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/mosaicism/">mosaicism</a>. Note: Testing of parental leukocyte DNA may not detect all instances of <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/somatic-mosaicism/">somatic mosaicism</a> and will not detect a pathogenic variant that is present in the germ cells only.</div></li></ul></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Although most individuals diagnosed with FH have an affected parent, the family history may appear to be negative because of failure to recognize the disorder in family members, lack of knowledge regarding family member's cholesterol levels and heart disease history, or late onset of the disorder in the affected parent. If <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> family members have been on statins for many years, there may not be a prominent family history of premature CAD. Therefore, an apparently negative family history cannot be confirmed until appropriate evaluations have been performed.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The parents of an individual with <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> pathogenic variants in <i>APOB</i>, <i>LDLR</i>, or <i>PCSK9</i> are obligate heterozygotes and are thus presumed to have FH [<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.mabuchi.2014.54">Mabuchi et al 2014</a>].</div></li></ul><p><b>Sibs of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a>.</b> The risk to the sibs of the proband depends on the genetic status of the proband's parents:</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div class="half_rhythm">If one parent of the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> is affected and/or is known to have the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> identified in the proband, the risk to the sibs of having FH is 50%.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div class="half_rhythm">If both parents of the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> are affected with FH and are known to have an FH-related <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>, there is a 50% risk that sibs will inherit one pathogenic variant and have FH, a 25% risk that sibs will inherit two pathogenic variants and have severe FH, and a 25% chance that sibs will inherit neither of the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/familial/">familial</a> pathogenic variants.</div><div class="half_rhythm">The severity of HoFH varies. HoFH caused by <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/homozygous/">homozygous</a> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/loss-of-function/">loss-of-function</a> <i>LDLR</i> pathogenic variants is typically associated with the most severe presentation, followed by HoFH caused by <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/compound-heterozygous/">compound heterozygous</a> <i>LDLR</i> pathogenic variants [<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.cuchel.2014.2146">Cuchel et al 2014</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.bertolini.2020.72">Bertolini et al 2020</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div class="half_rhythm">If the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> has a known FH-related <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> that cannot be detected in the leukocyte DNA of either parent, the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/recurrence-risk/">recurrence risk</a> to sibs is estimated to be 1% because of the theoretic possibility of parental <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/germline-mosaicism/">germline mosaicism</a> [<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.rahbari.2016.126">Rahbari et al 2016</a>]. All sibs should undergo predictive genetic testing and cholesterol screening.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div class="half_rhythm">If the parents are clinically unaffected but their genetic status is unknown, sibs should still be considered at risk for FH because of the possibility of reduced <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/penetrance/">penetrance</a> in a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> parent or the theoretic possibility of parental <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/germline-mosaicism/">germline mosaicism</a>. All sibs should undergo predictive genetic testing and cholesterol screening.</div></li></ul><p>
<b>Offspring of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a></b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Each child of an individual with FH caused by a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> in <i>APOB</i>, <i>LDLR</i>, or <i>PCSK9</i> has a 50% chance of inheriting the pathogenic variant and having FH.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>All children of an individual with HoFH (i.e., <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> FH-related pathogenic variants) will inherit a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> and have FH.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If the reproductive partner of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> is <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an FH-related <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> in the same <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a> as the proband or a different FH-related gene, offspring are at risk of inheriting two pathogenic variants and having severe FH. Cholesterol screening &#x02013; followed by <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genetic-counseling/">genetic counseling</a> and genetic testing if cholesterol levels and family history are concerning for FH &#x02013; should be offered to the reproductive partner to assess this risk. (FH is more common in several populations [see <a href="#hyperchol.Prevalence">Prevalence</a>] as a result of founder variants.)</div></li></ul><p><b>Other family members.</b> The risk to other family members depends on the status of the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a>'s parents: if one or both parents have an FH-related <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>, sibs and parents of the proband's parents are at 50% risk of having a pathogenic variant and, thus, FH.</p></div><div id="hyperchol.LDLRAP1Related_Autosomal_Reces"><h3><i>LDLRAP1</i>-Related Autosomal Recessive FH &#x02013; Risk to Family Members</h3><p>
<b>Parents of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a></b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The parents of an individual with <i>LDLRAP1</i>-related FH are presumed to be <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for one <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Molecular genetic testing is recommended for the parents of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> to confirm that both parents are <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an <i>LDLRAP1</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> and to allow reliable <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/recurrence-risk/">recurrence risk</a> assessment.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> is detected in only one parent and parental identity testing has confirmed biological maternity and paternity, it is possible that one of the pathogenic variants identified in the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> occurred as a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/de-novo/"><i>de novo</i></a> event in the proband or as a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/postzygotic/">postzygotic</a> <i>de novo</i> event in a mosaic parent [<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.j_nsson.2017.519">J&#x000f3;nsson et al 2017</a>]. If the proband appears to have <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/homozygous/">homozygous</a> (i.e., the same two) pathogenic variants, additional possibilities to consider include:</div><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>A single- or multiexon <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/deletion/">deletion</a> in the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> that was not detected by <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/sequence-analysis/">sequence analysis</a> and that resulted in the artifactual appearance of homozygosity;</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Uniparental isodisomy for the parental <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/chromosome/">chromosome</a> with the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> that resulted in homozygosity for the pathogenic variant in the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a>.</div></li></ul></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Individuals who are <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an <i>LDLRAP1</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> (i.e., carriers) are typically asymptomatic.</div></li></ul><p>
<b>Sibs of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a></b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If both parents are known to be <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an <i>LDLRAP1</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>, each sib of an affected individual has at conception a 25% chance of being affected, a 50% chance of being a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/carrier/">carrier</a>, and a 25% chance of inheriting neither of the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/familial/">familial</a> pathogenic variants.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Individuals who are <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an <i>LDLRAP1</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> (i.e., carriers) are typically asymptomatic.</div></li></ul><p>
<b>Offspring of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a></b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The offspring of an individual with <i>LDLRAP1</i>-related FH are obligate heterozygotes (carriers) for a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> in <i>LDLRAP1</i> (individuals who are <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an <i>LDLRAP1</i> pathogenic variant are typically asymptomatic).</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If the reproductive partner of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> is <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an <i>LDLRAP1</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>, offspring are at risk of inheriting <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> pathogenic variants and having <i>LDLRAP1</i>-related FH. Genetic counseling and genetic testing can be offered to the reproductive partner to assess this risk.</div></li></ul><p><b>Other family members.</b> Each sib of the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a>'s parents is at a 50% risk of being a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/carrier/">carrier</a> of an <i>LDLRAP1</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>.</p><div id="hyperchol.Carrier_Detection"><h4>Carrier Detection</h4><p>Carrier testing for at-risk relatives requires prior identification of the <i>LDLRAP1</i> pathogenic variants in the family.</p></div></div><div id="hyperchol.Related_Genetic_Counseling_Iss"><h3>Related Genetic Counseling Issues</h3><p><b>Family-based cascade screening.</b> The CDC has classified FH as a Tier 1 condition, indicating a significant benefit from performing family-based cascade screening. Cholesterol testing, with or without molecular testing in relatives of affected persons with FH, can be used to identify individuals with FH and provide them with lifesaving treatment. Early diagnosis and treatment of relatives at risk for FH can reduce morbidity and mortality [<a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.knowles.2017.381">Knowles et al 2017</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.sturm.2018.662">Sturm et al 2018</a>].</p><p><b>Predictive genetic testing</b> for asymptomatic family members at risk for FH requires prior identification of the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>(s) in the family. If the FH-related pathogenic variant(s) in the family are known, it is recommended that all first-degree family members be offered predictive genetic testing.</p><p>See Management, <a href="#hyperchol.Evaluation_of_Relatives_at_Ris">Evaluation of Relatives at Risk</a> for information on evaluating at-risk relatives for the purpose of early diagnosis and treatment.</p><p>
<b>Family planning</b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Young women with FH should receive counseling regarding contraindications of lipid-lowering therapies during pregnancy (see Management, <a href="#hyperchol.Pregnancy_Management">Pregnancy Management</a>).</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a>'s reproductive partner should be offered cholesterol screening, followed by <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genetic-counseling/">genetic counseling</a> and genetic testing if cholesterol levels and family history are concerning for FH. Ideally, this should be offered prior to conception to assess the risk of having a child with HoFH.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The optimal time for determination of genetic risk and discussion of the availability of prenatal/<a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/preimplantation-genetic-testing/">preimplantation genetic testing</a> is before pregnancy.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>It is appropriate to offer <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genetic-counseling/">genetic counseling</a> (including discussion of potential risks to offspring and reproductive options) to young adults who are affected or at risk.</div></li></ul><p><b>DNA banking.</b> Because it is likely that testing methodology and our understanding of genes, pathogenic mechanisms, and diseases will improve in the future, consideration should be given to banking DNA from probands in whom a molecular diagnosis has not been confirmed (i.e., the causative pathogenic mechanism is unknown). For more information, see <a class="bk_pop" href="#hyperchol.REF.huang.2022.389">Huang et al [2022]</a>.</p></div><div id="hyperchol.Prenatal_Testing_and_Preimplan"><h3>Prenatal Testing and Preimplantation Genetic Testing</h3><p>Once the FH-causing <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>(s) have been identified in an affected family member, prenatal and <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/preimplantation-genetic-testing/">preimplantation genetic testing</a> are possible.</p><p>Differences in perspective may exist among medical professionals and within families regarding the use of prenatal and <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/preimplantation-genetic-testing/">preimplantation genetic testing</a>. While most health care professionals would consider use of prenatal and preimplantation genetic testing to be a personal decision, discussion of these issues may be helpful.</p></div></div><div id="hyperchol.Resources"><h2 id="_hyperchol_Resources_">Resources</h2><p>

organizations.
